Component library.

Every building block the Jobility site needs, live, with its class names and hooks, when to use it, which pages use it, and what not to do. Built for Nasir to rebuild the remaining pages in WordPress and Divi without guessing. 100 components, 63 of them new for the copy-only pages.

How to use it

  1. Find the page in Page templates at the end. Each map lists the blocks top to bottom; click one to jump to it.
  2. Build each block from its entry: the live example, then Markup under it for the exact HTML. Every entry shows desktop, and a phone version in a real 390px frame wherever the layout changes.
  3. Style only through assets/site.css (sections 1 to 14). Add a modifier class rather than a page-specific copy; a forked style is a future mismatch.
  4. Behavior comes from data attributes read by assets/site.js (and offering.js for the three offering signatures, home-hero.js for the home hero). fd- classes are stable names for Divi modules and analytics; don’t rename them.
  5. Prices come from one place, FD.PRICES in site.js. data-price="post.labor" fills a number; the typed number is the fallback.
  6. Nothing marked unfinished ships. A dashed underline with a Q tag is wording to confirm; a dashed box is a held line. Fill or delete each before launch.

At a glance

Type
Figtree (stand-in for Riposte), 68 / 56 / 36 / 26 / 19 / 15. Body 19px, 17px on phones.
Color
Night Shift ink and the one dark band; Day Shift bands; Passion for every action.
Radii
14 panels, 10 buttons and inputs, 6 chips.
Layout
1320px row, 32px gutter (20 on phones). Stacks at 1200, 900 and 700px; tables at 480.
Standards
WCAG 2.2 AA, 44px touch targets, visible focus (Night Shift ring with an Optimism halo), reduced motion respected, noindex on the prototype.
Legend
New in this library built for the copy-only pages. From the built pages already live on Home or an offering page.

Foundations

Tokens live at the top of site.css (section 1). Change a value there and every page follows. Never paste a hex value into a Divi module; use the variable.

Color roles and contrast

From the built pages
Classes
--night --day --passion --passion-dk --purpose --energy --optimism --ink-2 --edge --line --mist .on-dark .on-day
Use when
Every surface and text color. Roles are fixed: Passion is for actions, Night Shift is the ink and the one dark band, Day Shift is a warm band, never the page base.
Used on
Every page.

Do

  • Body text in --ink-2 on white or Day Shift; headings in Night Shift.
  • Links on a Day Shift band in Night Shift, underlined (.on-day does this).
  • On Night Shift, use Optimism for the focus ring and Day Shift for secondary text.

Don’t

  • Passion text on Day Shift (4.37:1) or on Night Shift (2.62:1).
  • Purpose or Energy for any word of text. Purpose is the j’s dot; Energy is the headline stop and the ribbon.
  • Day Shift as a full-page background, or a gradient anywhere except the brand line.

Desktop

  • #1F2C41Night Shift--nightInk, the hero, the one dark band
  • #F6E5DADay Shift--dayWarm bands and surfaces
  • #D3005BPassion--passionEvery action: buttons, links
  • #B8004FPassion, dark--passion-dkHover, error text
  • #FF3B58Purpose--purposeThe j’s dot only
  • #FF6634Energy--energyHeadline stop and the ribbon
  • #FF9241Optimism--optimismRibbon start, focus halo
  • #4A5466Ink 2--ink-2Body text
  • #8A8F99Edge--edgeControl borders (3.25:1, non-text)
  • #E3DCD6Line--lineHairlines
  • #FBF4EFMist--mistTable highlights, quiet fills
Measured contrast (WCAG 2.2). AA needs 4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for large text and for control edges.
PairRatioUse

AaInk 2 on Day Shift6.23:1Pass. Body on warm bands

Type scale

From the built pages
Classes
.h-page .h-sec .h-sub .h-item .lede .small .micro .stop .num
Use when
Every heading and paragraph. One family (Figtree, standing in for Riposte until Jobility licenses it), weight carries the hierarchy, sentence case, no eyebrows except on ad landing pages.
Used on
Every page.
Sizes
68 / 56 / 36 / 26 / 19 / 15. Fluid between phone and desktop with clamp(). Body 19px desktop, 17px phone (Parmi asked for larger supporting text). Only product-view labels go to 14.

Do

  • One .h-page (the h1) per page. Section headings .h-sec, sub-sections .h-sub.
  • Self-host Figtree as a woff2 subset with font-display: swap in the handoff.

Don’t

  • Tracked uppercase labels or eyebrows above headings.
  • Monospace or a second family for numbers; use .num (tabular figures).

Desktop

.h-page · 68 / 0.98 · 800 · -0.032em · phone 44

Hire vetted people.

.h-sec · 56 / 1.0 · 800 · phone 36

Which plan fits you?

.h-sub · 36 / 1.08 · 800 · phone 28

Your short list, already screened.

.h-item · 26 / 1.18 · 800 · phone 22

Want to pay only if you hire?

.lede · 19 / 1.55 · 400 · max 56ch · phone 18

Post a job once and get a short list of screened candidates, not a pile of resumes.

body · 19 / 1.55 · phone 17

Jobility finds, vets and shortlists candidates. You hire them onto your own payroll.

.small, source lines, captions · 15 / 1.5

Source: Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms, 2021 data. 20% was the most common direct-hire fee; firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.

.num · 800, tabular figures

$3,600

Spacing, layout and radii

From the built pages
Classes
.wrap .sec .sec--tight .sec-head .split .split--even --gutter --sec --maxw --r --r-s --r-xs --shadow
Use when
Every section. .wrap is the Divi row: 1320px max, 32px gutter (20px on phones). .sec is the Divi section: 72 to 128px of padding.
Used on
Every page.
Breakpoints
1280: the nav collapses to Menu. 1200: two-column layouts stack. 900: three-column rows stack. 700: phone. 480: tables stack into labeled pairs.

Do

  • One radius family: 14px panels and cards, 10px buttons, inputs and rows inside panels, 6px chips.
  • Only panels that float over something (the hero panel, the offer letter, the pay stub, product views) carry --shadow.

Don’t

  • Shadows on ordinary cards, hover lift, or a fourth radius.
  • Centered text, except inside the final CTA band.

Desktop

--gutter 20px phone
--gutter 32px
.sec-head margin, 36 to 56px
.split gap, 40 to 88px
--sec section padding, 72 to 128px
14px--rpanels, cards
10px--r-sbuttons, inputs
6px--r-xschips
Shadow--shadowfloating panels only

  <div class="lib-bar" style="--w:56px"><span></span><code>.sec-head</code> margin, 36 to 56px</div>

The brand line, the stop, and the two waivers

From the built pages
Classes
.stop .fd-bline .fd-draw .fd-node .fd-dot #fd-rib #fd-rib-h #fd-rib-v
Use when
The brand line is the logo’s j drawn as one stroke. It is the only ornament on the site: one signature per page, plus the loop inside photo placeholders.
Used on
Home hero, the switch, each offering signature (offer letter, pay stub, shift card), photo placeholders.
Waivers (do not flag)
1. A page or section headline may end on an Energy-colored stop, as the brand guide sets “Jobility.” It is punctuation, never a single accented word. 2. The ribbon gradient (Optimism, Energy, Purpose, Passion) lives only along the brand line.
Contrast note
Energy on white is 2.92:1. The stop passes only because it is a period that carries no meaning; the sentence reads complete without its color.

Do

  • Draw the line in front of the cards it joins, with Night Shift beads where it touches each item and the Purpose dot above the stem, as in the mark.
  • Use the gradient defs #fd-rib, #fd-rib-h (horizontal) and #fd-rib-v (vertical) once per page.

Don’t

  • A second brand line in the same view, a line that loops for decoration, or a line over a face in a photo.
  • The gradient on buttons, backgrounds, text or borders.
  • Colored stops on more than one heading per screen view.

Desktop

Post your first job.

The stroke, its beads and the dot. The ribbon runs Optimism at the start to Passion at the end.

Markup
<div class="lib-brand">
  <p class="h-sec">Post your first job<span class="stop">.</span></p>

Placeholder conventions: .confirm, .held and photo frames

L12From the built pages
Classes
.confirm .held .ph .ph--portrait .ph--wide .ph--dark .ph__cap .screen
Hooks
data-q="Q2" title="To confirm with Jobility: Q1"
Use when
Any wording Jobility has not confirmed ({{CONFIRM Qn}} in the copy deck), any line held until a question is answered, and every photo or product view still owed.
Used on
Every page, until each question is closed.
Rule
The page reads clean by default: .confirm is plain text and .held is hidden. Review mode (see the next entry) shows both. Before launch, every .confirm is replaced by the answered wording and every .held is either filled or deleted.
Example views (R7)
One string, one style, under every example object: .ex-note “Example view. Names and numbers are made up.” No “Example view” chips in the object headers, no other wording.

Do

  • Keep the Q number in data-q.
  • Name exactly what is owed in the photo caption (subject, setting, light, license).
  • One placeholder style site-wide: Day fill, hairline, shot-brief caption, nothing drawn inside.

Don’t

  • A stock or AI-generated face standing in for a named person: Parmi’s portrait stays this placeholder until his photo arrives. Working-moment photos use .photo (Photography group).
  • The brand line or any other art inside a placeholder.
  • .confirm inside an h1 or h2. Flag the line beneath instead.

Desktop

[First and last name], [role]. The person customers can ask for.

Turn on review mode (bottom-left) to see the dashed marks and the held line.

Held until Q14: one of the two fixed guarantee wordings, never improvised.

Markup
<div class="lib-ph">
  <div>
    <p><span class="confirm" data-q="Q2" title="To confirm with Jobility: Q2, the named contact">[First and last name], [role]</span>. The person customers can ask for.</p>
    <p class="small" style="margin-top:12px">Turn on review mode (bottom-left) to see the dashed marks and the held line.</p>
    <p class="held" data-q="Q14" style="margin-top:16px">Held until Q14: one of the two fixed guarantee wordings, never improvised.</p>
  </div>
  <figure class="ph ph--wide" aria-label="Placeholder for a photograph">
    <figcaption class="ph__cap"><b>Photo: a worker checking the app on a break</b><span class="qonly">Photo owed. Real workplace, natural light, face visible, mid-task (image 6).</span></figcaption>
  </figure>
</div>

Review mode: toggle, Q chips, section footnote and page callout

New in this library
Classes
.qtoggle .qbar .show-q .confirm .qchip .qnote .qcallout .held .qonly
Hooks
data-q ?review=1 sessionStorage fd-review
Use when
Lets Jobility review a clean page, then switch on the marks. site.js injects the toggle (“Review: N lines wait on Jobility. Show”) at the bottom left, above the phone bar on phones, and toggles body.show-q.
Used on
Every page with a data-q on it.
Authoring
One .qchip per question per section (the first .confirm for that Q). One .qnote at the foot of each section listing its open questions; the held wording goes in its title, not inline. .qcallout once per page, near the top, only where one answer decides most of the page (none on the pages since the Oct 21 answers).
Never
A .confirm inside h1 or h2. A held box inside a table. A Q chip on every row.
Built pages (integration)
Every page in the site plan is coded: index, direct-hiring, contingent-labor, gig-work (and ?v=healthcare), contact, pricing, compare-staffing-agency, how-it-works, faq, for-workers, about-us, login, privacy-policy, hirer-terms, gigee-terms, accessibility, 404, lp-hire-without-agency-fees, lp-post-a-job (held until Q4, linked from nowhere) and this library. Links between them open the page; the “This page is in the copy document” note now fires only for a bare # demo link or a host outside the portal, the worker login, the two stores and jobilitytalent.com.

Do

  • Put wholly held FAQ answers on the item itself: <div class="faq-item held" data-q="Q14">, so the clean page never shows an empty answer.

Don’t

  • Reword an unconfirmed claim so it no longer needs a mark.

Clean wording: data-clean, .qonly and real links

L12New in this library
Classes
.confirm .qonly
Hooks
data-clean="buyer-safe text" data-clean="" (review-only clause) a[data-topic="direct|temp|shift|plan"] data-orig (set by site.js)
Use when
The clean page (review off) must never show a bracket, a slot name or a production note. Any .confirm whose words are a stand-in carries data-clean: neutral wording that is true today. site.js shows it when review is off and restores the original (kept in data-orig) when review is on. Production notes (photo briefs, image numbers, supply items, recordings) sit in .qonly and show only in review.
Used on
Every page. Examples on the offering pages: “Short list in [n] days” reads “A short list first, then interviews”; “states or metros” reads “Ask us where we staff”; “Jobility’s policy” for workers’ comp reads “Ask us”; a clause with data-clean="" (“, plus workers’ comp”) disappears.
Punctuation
Keep trailing punctuation inside the .confirm span; site.js also moves a stop that directly follows a span inside it, so the review chip never splits a word from its period.
Links
Talk to us and Get a quote: <a data-topic="direct" href="contact.html?topic=direct"> (temp on Contingent labor, shift on Gig work, plan for “talk to sales”). Every Post button (Post a job, Post a temp job, Post a shift) is the plain portal sign-up, https://enterprise.jobility.com/signup, with no query parameters. No phone links anywhere: Jobility shows no phone number, so every phone label became “Talk to us”. Pages not in the prototype keep their real slug; site.js intercepts the click with a short note.
Capture check
On the clean page, visible text has no [, {{, “owed”, “Kim’s” or “supply item”, no phone wording, no worker-classification wording, no ID-check claim, and no phone link or sign-up URL with parameters.

Do

  • Write clean wording that is true whatever Jobility answers.
  • Use one data-clean per stand-in; the outer link carries the accessible label.

Don’t

  • A clean value that promises a number or a place Jobility has not confirmed.
  • Deleting the original wording: review mode still needs it.

Desktop

Short list in [n] days

Available in: states or metros

Talk to us Post a temp job

Jobility runs payroll and taxes, plus workers’ comp.

Turn on review mode to see the original wording come back.

Header and utility bar

L1From the built pages
Classes
.site-util .site-nav .nav-links .nl .ns .nav-cta .btn--post .menu-btn .menu-panel .on-dark
Hooks
data-menu data-menu-btn data-head-post data-hero .is-scrolled .is-filled .is-open
Use when
Every page except the ad landing pages. Light variant on white heroes; add .on-dark to both bars over the Night Shift home hero.
Used on
Home, the three offering pages, Pricing, Compare, How it works, FAQ, For workers, About, Talk to us, Log in, legal, Accessibility, 404.
Behavior
Sticky. A hairline appears once scrolled. Post a job is outlined while the element marked data-hero is on screen and fills after, so the hero’s button is the only filled one in view. Below 1280px the links collapse to Menu; Escape closes it and returns focus.
One header on every page (integration)
All 17 site pages carry the same header markup, written by _lib/integrate.py from index.html. It sits in one display:contents wrapper: <div class="hd"> on light pages, <div class="hd hd-dark on-dark"> on Home and the three offering pages (Night heroes). Only two things vary: the wrapper’s theme class, and aria-current="page" on any header or footer link to the page itself (nav, menu, utility bar, footer; never the logo), styled with the Passion underline. The menu’s “See prices” goes to pricing.html everywhere. The Talk to us topic is direct in the markup; a page that sells another mode sets <body data-page-topic="temp"> (Contingent labor) or "shift" (Gig work) and site.js re-points the header and footer Talk to us links. In Divi this is the one global header plus a per-page body attribute. LP pages keep the no-nav .lp-head.

Do

  • Keep the visible second line under the three offering links; it is part of the link text.
  • The same pair on every page (R7): outlined Talk to us (the page’s topic) and Post a job (data-head-post, fills once the hero’s primary has left). The page’s own primary lives in its hero, so the global nav never changes shape.

Don’t

  • Hover tooltips or dropdowns for the offerings.
  • A second filled primary button in the header while the hero is in view.

How it works (integration)

Primary (Passion, filled) for the page’s one main action; secondary (outlined) beside it; the header’s Post a job uses .btn--post.

Every page.
Labels
Post a job · Post a temp job · Post a shift · See prices · Talk to us · Get a quote · See available times · Send message · Book a call · Compare the cost · Get the app · Log in to hire. Every Post label goes to the portal sign-up with no parameters. No phone label: Jobility shows no phone number.

  • 48px tall (44px for .btn--sm). On phones a button row stacks full width.

Don’t

  • “Request a demo”, “Get started”, “Learn more”, “Contact us”, “See pricing”.
  • Gradient, shadow or icon-only buttons.

Forms

One form on the site (Talk to us) plus the calculator inputs. Every field has a visible label, errors are plain sentences beside the field, and free email addresses are welcome: many of the owners Jobility wants use Gmail.

Text fields, select, text area, checkbox and their states

L17From the built pages
Classes
.field .label .input .select .textarea .hint .opt-tag .checkbox .err .suggest .is-invalid
Hooks
aria-invalid="true" aria-describedby data-err data-suggest
Use when
Any input. Labels sit above, hints under the label text, the error under the field.
Used on
Talk to us, the LP2 form, the calculator, the quick quote.
Error wording
“Please enter an email address, like name@company.com.” “Please enter a 5-digit ZIP code.” “Please enter your name.” Shown on leaving a field (only once it has been typed in) and on submit; cleared as soon as it is fixed.

Do

  • Mark the optional field “(optional)”; everything else shown is required.
  • Use autocomplete tokens (name, email, organization, postal-code, tel).

Don’t

  • Placeholder text as the label.
  • “Work email only” or blocking Gmail, Yahoo or Outlook addresses.
  • Red-only error signals; the message and the icon carry it.

Desktop

Any address works, including Gmail.

L17From the built pages
Classes
.choices .choice .choice--card .cards .seg
Hooks
data-seg role="radiogroup" role="radio" aria-checked fd:seg (event)
Use when
Chips for short options (role type in the quick quote). Cards when each option needs a second line (the Talk to us topic). The segmented control for two or three views of the same thing (8% | 15%, With Jobility | Hiring temps yourself).
Used on
Home, Direct hiring and Pricing (role chips), Talk to us (cards), Pricing cards, Compare (segmented control on phones), Contingent labor (pay stub).
Keyboard
Chips and cards are native radio inputs. The segmented control is a radiogroup of buttons: arrows, Home and End move the choice; it fires fd:seg with the value.

Do

  • Selected state is a full Night Shift fill with white text, never color alone.

Don’t

  • A select menu that hides five or more choices people need to compare.

Talk to us form

New in this library
Classes
.tform .cards .cards--more .tform-row .tform-note .tform-submit .tform-bullets .reach .thanks .ct .ct-grid .ct-sum .ct-req .req
Hooks
data-tform data-show="hiring plan other worker press" data-more data-err data-summary data-suggest data-sms data-thanks data-for data-push="/demo-request/thanks/" ?topic=plan data-tf-sum data-tf-sum-dl FD.tfSummary()
Use when
The site’s only form. The first choice decides what the rest asks: hiring choices lead to the scheduler, everything else to the right inbox.
Used on
contact.html in this prototype (the live slug stays /demo-request/); LP2 hero with the topic preset (on hold).
Required
Hiring path: six fields (topic, name, email, company, how many people, ZIP). Subscription path swaps “how many people” for “jobs a month”. Other path: four (topic, name, email, message). Phone and SMS consent are optional; the SMS box appears only once a phone number is typed.
Submit
Hiring: “See available times”. Anything else: “Send message”. On a server-confirmed hiring submit, fire generate_lead (never on the click), render the thank-you in place and push /demo-request/thanks/. The scheduler embed loads only after submit. Non-hiring submits fire contact_submitted and are never an Ads conversion.
Try it
Submit empty to see the error summary; type “name@gmial.com” in Email to see the typo hint; choose Something else, then I work through Jobility.
contact.html (R9)
One screen in the site chrome (light header, the shared footer, noindex). The form reads topic, role, zip, len, n, start, end, src and utm_* from the URL: the topic card is chosen, ZIP and “how many people” are filled, the optional details box gets one pre-written line (“Shift rate request. Role: CNA. Shift length: 12 hours.”), and the rest rides in hidden fields. A Day card, What our team receives, mirrors the request as it changes. Required fields carry a Passion asterisk and the required attribute. Submit shows “Thanks. We’ll be in touch.” in place with no network call and no URL push (the live form pushes /demo-request/thanks/). Unconfirmed lines (the 20-minute call, the one-business-day reply, “We’ll never share your details”) are review chips with a neutral clean wording; a review-only note says the form is not wired. Phone and SMS consent are held until Q18.

Do

  • Keep the hidden attribution fields (UTM, landing page, gclid, gbraid, wbraid, fbclid, li_fat_id).
  • Route “I work through Jobility” to support, never to sales.

Don’t

  • A nine-option select for the topic.
  • A map, or a phone number (Jobility shows none). The address text does the trust work.
  • Live chat at launch.

    Alerts

    Do

    Log in chooser

    New in this library
    Classes
    .login .login-card .trouble
    Use when
    The /login/ page: send hirers and workers to the right portal, and answer the app’s top login complaints under the worker card.
    Used on
    Log in /login/. Tracking: login_click with audience.
    login.html (integration)
    Built as login.html: two cards (I’m hiring to enterprise.jobility.com/login; I’m a worker to gigee.jobility.com/login and the app), then the worker troubleshooting list. data-track="login_click" with data-audience.

    Do

    • Keep the note “The hiring portal works best on a computer.”
    • Send verification email from a jobility.com address with SPF, DKIM and DMARC before this ships (Q10).

    Don’t

    • One shared login button that guesses the audience.

    Desktop

    I’m hiring.

    Go to your hiring portal.

    Log in to hire

    The hiring dashboard works best on a computer.

    I’m a worker.

    Open the Jobility app, or log in on the web.

    Download on theApp StoreGet it onGoogle Play

    Log in on the web

    Didn’t get the verification email?
    Check your spam folder for a message from our jobility.com sending address. We only send links to jobility.com addresses.
    The app doesn’t recognize your email?
    Use the email you signed up with. Still stuck? Email support inbox or call support number.
    Want to delete your account?
    Email support inbox and we’ll delete it.

    Phone, 390px

    • Cards stack, hirer first.

    Content blocks

    The sections pages are made of. Each one has a single job; most are typographic rows split by hairlines rather than card grids.

    Section frame, headings and bands

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .sec .sec--tight .sec-head .split .split--even .on-day .on-dark .cta-band
    Use when
    Every section: section.sec > .wrap > content. Alternate white and Day Shift bands; Night Shift appears once per page, for the final ask (and the home hero).
    Used on
    Every page.

    Do

    • Two white sections in a row get a hairline between them automatically.
    • .split puts the heading left (5 columns) and the content right (7) on desktop.

    Don’t

    • Two Night Shift bands on one page.
    • Section fade-ins or reveals on scroll.

    What you use now, and what changes with Jobility

    Page hero for copy-only pages

    New in this library
    Classes
    .pg-hero .crumbs .stamp .jumps .btn-row .micro
    Use when
    The first screen of every page that is not Home, an offering or an ad landing page: H1 (from the deck, word for word), one subhead, at most two buttons, an optional stamp and jump links.
    Used on
    Pricing, Compare, How it works, FAQ, For workers (on Day Shift), About, Talk to us, Log in, legal pages, Accessibility.
    Variants
    Compare adds the “Fees last reviewed” stamp; FAQ and legal pages add “Last updated” (it must match dateModified in the schema); How it works makes the jump links sticky.

    Do

    • Keep the subhead under about 30 words on pages people arrive at from ads or search.

    Don’t

    • A photo or illustration in this hero; the type and the numbers do the work.
    • Eyebrow labels above the H1.

    Desktop

    1. Home
    2. Jobility vs. a staffing agency

    A staffing agency alternative for direct hires: what you pay and what you get.

    Fees last reviewed: October 2026

    Both can find you vetted people. In a 2021 industry survey, 20% of first-year salary was the most common agency direct-hire fee, and for it the agency runs the search. Jobility’s placement fee is 15% when we do the work and send you a candidate, or 8% when you post and schedule yourself.

    Post a jobSee prices

    Post free on the placement-fee plan and pay only when you hire. Or pay per post from $150, with no fee when you hire.

    • How agencies charge
    • Side by side
    • Fee math
    • What an agency fee buys
    • When an agency fits
    • Questions

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • H1 drops to 44px; buttons stack full width.
    • Jump links wrap; not sticky.

    Desktop

    For businesses hiring

    Hire full-time staff for a placement fee of 8% or 15%.

    Post free. We find and screen candidates. You pay only when you hire: 8% if you post and schedule, 15% if we do the work and send you a candidate.

    Post a job

    Post free on the placement-fee plan.

    One $45,000 hire

    • Common agency fee, 20%$9,000
    • Jobility managed, 15%. We do the work and send you a candidate.$6,750
    • Jobility self-service, 8%. You post and schedule.$3,600

    Source: Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms, 2021 data. 20% was the most common direct-hire fee; firms commonly charged 15 to 25%. Jobility fees are a percentage of first-year salary.

    Steps

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .steps .step .step-n .steps-after .steps--four
    Use when
    A real sequence of three (four for workers). Static: steps never animate.
    Used on
    Direct hiring, Contingent labor, Gig work (slot B); For workers (four steps, .steps--four, new); LP1 (three one-line steps).

    Do

    • Numbers are aria-hidden; the list is an ol.

    Don’t

    • Icons per step, or a connecting line.

    Desktop

    1. 1

      Download the app and build your profile.

      Your skills, experience, availability and how far you’ll travel.

    2. 2

      Get verified once.

      We check your skills, availability and work history.

    3. 3

      Get matched.

      We send you shifts and jobs that fit. Accept the ones you want.

    4. 4

      Work and get paid.

      Clock in and out in the app. Pay arrives on a set schedule.

    Timeline

    New in this library
    Classes
    .tline .t-k .is-key .tline--compact
    Use when
    Things that happen in order over time: what happens after you post (and when you pay), the next steps after booking a call, and company history once the founding year is confirmed.
    Used on
    How it works #when-you-pay; Talk to us thank-you; About (history, held until supply item 16).

    Do

    • Mark the item the reader is waiting for with .is-key (a filled bead).

    Don’t

    • The brand line or the ribbon as the timeline rail.
    • Dates that are not confirmed.

    Desktop

    1. Step 1

      You post. Your job goes live.

    2. Step 2

      Matches arrive in your portal

      Screened against your requirements. We email you when the first ones are ready.

    3. Step 3

      You choose: interview, shortlist or pass.

    4. Step 4

      You hire.

      Direct hires join your payroll. Temp and shift workers can be employed by Jobility.

    5. When you pay

      By plan

      Per post: when you post it. Placement fee: only after you hire, on the date set in your terms. Temp and shift workers: by the hour, at the rate we quote before anyone starts.

    History variant, held until Jobility confirms the founding year (supply item 16): the same list with years in .t-k.

    Term rows

    New in this library
    Classes
    .deflist
    Use when
    A short term and its explanation, many times: how agencies charge, when you pay plan by plan, company facts, legal definitions on a page that is not legal text.
    Used on
    Compare #agency-fees, Pricing #when-you-pay, About (company facts), How it works (standard vs extra).

    Do

    • Every number travels with its date in the same sentence.

    Don’t

    • A blank cell or a dash where an answer is missing; hold the row instead.

    Desktop

    Direct hire: a one-time fee
    Usually a percentage of the person’s first-year salary. In a Staffing Industry Analysts survey (2021 data), 20% was the most common agency direct-hire fee, and firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.
    Temp and contract: a markup on hourly pay
    The agency employs the worker and bills you their pay plus a markup. The markup varies by role and region; ask your agency for the bill rate and the pay rate.
    What the fee usually buys
    A recruiter who runs the search and often the interviews, and a replacement guarantee if the hire leaves early, typically 30 to 90 days.
    Converting a temp to staff
    Many agencies charge a fee when you hire a temp permanently.

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • Term above its explanation.
    Markup
    <dl class="deflist">
  <dt>Direct hire: a one-time fee</dt><dd>Usually a percentage of the person's first-year salary. In a Staffing Industry Analysts survey (2021 data), 20% was the most common agency direct-hire fee, and firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.</dd>
  <dt>Temp and contract: a markup on hourly pay</dt><dd>The agency employs the worker and bills you their pay plus a markup. The markup varies by role and region; ask your agency for the bill rate and the pay rate.</dd>
  <dt>What the fee usually buys</dt><dd>A recruiter who runs the search and often the interviews, and a replacement guarantee if the hire leaves early, typically 30 to 90 days.</dd>
  <dt>Converting a temp to staff</dt><dd>Many agencies charge a fee when you hire a temp permanently.</dd>

    Short answer and pulled quote

    New in this library
    Classes
    .answer .pull .who
    Use when
    The short answer is written to be quoted by AI assistants and snippets: each sentence stands alone with its own figure and date. The pulled quote is Parmi’s line with his name, for pages too light for the founder block.
    Used on
    Compare (short answer), Pricing #vs-agency, About (direct hiring vs RPO lead answer); LP1 (pulled quote).

    Do

    • First sentence names Jobility.
    • Source line directly under.

    Don’t

    • Pronouns that need the previous sentence (“It also...”).

    Desktop

    Jobility vs. a staffing agency, in short. Jobility is a direct hiring platform: it finds, vets and shortlists candidates, and you hire the person onto your own payroll. Jobility’s placement fee is 8% of first-year salary when you run the interviews yourself, or 15% when Jobility’s team manages the search, and it is charged only when you hire.

    Source: Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms, 2021 data. 20% was the most common direct-hire fee; firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.

    “I created Jobility to save businesses the cost and time of posting on multiple job boards and relying on costly staffing services.”

    Parmi Cheema, Founder and CEO

    Markup
    <div class="answer"><p><b>Jobility vs. a staffing agency, in short.</b> Jobility is a direct hiring platform: it finds, vets and shortlists candidates, and you hire the person onto your own payroll. Jobility's placement fee is 8% of first-year salary when you run the interviews yourself, or 15% when Jobility's team manages the search, and it is charged only when you hire.</p><p class="small">Source: Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms, 2021 data. 20% was the most common direct-hire fee; firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.</p></div>
<div class="pull" style="margin-top:36px"><blockquote><p>&ldquo;I created Jobility to save businesses the cost and time of posting on multiple job boards and relying on costly staffing services.&rdquo;</p></blockquote><p class="who"><b>Parmi Cheema</b>, Founder and CEO</p></div>

    When to choose which

    New in this library
    Classes
    .choose .is-us
    Use when
    Tell the reader when not to use Jobility. The page’s best trust and AI-answer asset.
    Used on
    Compare #when-agency; About (direct hiring vs direct sourcing vs RPO, as a plain list).

    Do

    • Name the sister company for full-service search.

    Don’t

    • Stacking the deck; every line must be something a fair agency would agree with.

    Desktop

    Choose an agency, or a full-service firm like Jobility Talent Solutions, if:

    • You want someone else to run the whole search, including the interviews.
    • You have no time to review candidates or interview in the weeks ahead.
    • The role is senior or rare enough that you want a recruiter approaching people who aren’t looking for work.

    Held until Q14: “You need a replacement guarantee and Jobility’s terms don’t give you one.”

    Choose Jobility if:

    • You can interview and make the offer yourself (self-service, 8%), or you want our team to manage the search for less than a typical agency fee (Jobility managed, 15%).
    • You want to see the price before you start.
    • You also need temp or shift workers, from the same account.

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • The two lists stack, agency first.

    Ways, segmented rows, quiet trio and plain list

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .ways .ways--two .ways--rows .w-link .w-extra .lead-trio .lead-trio--one .quiet .quiet--lead .plain-list .plain-list--sm
    Use when
    Ways: two parallel options with a link each. Segmented rows (.ways--rows): a menu of options that is not a sequence, one row each (name, line, link right-aligned), so non-sequences never look like the numbered steps. Quiet trio: three short ideas under a 2px rule; the lead idea leads by order and size, never by a filled card. Plain list: three short statements set large.
    Used on
    Gig work (segmented rows; one quiet item as a strip, .lead-trio--one; plain list), For workers (kinds of work), Pricing (temp and shift, .ways--two), About (plain list).

    • A lone Night Shift card among plain columns.

      How shifts work

    • Contracts.

      Weeks or months of cover, on Jobility’s payroll.

      Temp and contract workers

    • On your staff.

      Hire a nurse or aide directly, with a flat price per post or a placement fee of 8% or 15%.

      Direct hiring

    Try before you hire.

    Work with someone for a few shifts first. Hire a shift worker onto your staff with no conversion fee after 480 hours (about three months).

    Posted where workers look.

    Screened workers nearby see your shift in the app.

    Employer of record.

    Jobility can be the employer of record. Choose it when you post.

    • Contingent labor.

      One hourly rate per worker, covering their pay and Jobility’s employer costs. Quoted before anyone starts.

      Get a quote

      Running several sites? Ask about a custom plan.

    • Gig work.

      One hourly rate per shift, quoted per role, before anyone starts.

      Get a quote

    FAQ accordion and question groups

    L13From the built pages
    Classes
    .faq .faq-list .faq-item .faq-q .faq-t .faq-ico .faq-a .stamp .jumps
    Hooks
    data-accordion aria-expanded aria-controls
    Use when
    Every question block. Answers are in the HTML (closed by JS, open with no JS) so search engines and assistants can read them. The FAQ page repeats the block per group, each group with its anchor.
    Used on
    Home, offering pages, Pricing, Compare, How it works (six each, word for word from deck 5.8), FAQ page (five groups: #faq-about, #faq-pricing, #faq-hiring, #faq-employer, #faq-healthcare), For workers (worker questions).
    Rules
    Heading text is literal: “Frequently asked questions”. No answer goes live with an open question: at launch an answer with an open Q is held, not shipped half-written. FAQPage schema matches the visible questions.

    Do

    • First sentence of each answer names Jobility and stands alone.

    Don’t

    • Loading answers on click.
    • Opening more than the clicked item.

    Desktop

    Pricing

    Last updated 20 October 2026

    See all questions

    Jobility charges either a flat price per job post, from $150, or a placement fee of 8% or 15% that you pay only when you hire. Temp and shift workers are quoted by the hour.

    No. Jobility never charges per applicant or per click.

    No. A hire from a paid post owes no placement fee: you pay the post price once, when you publish.

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • Heading and links above the list.
    • Questions at 20px; the plus icon keeps a 28px circle inside a 64px row.

    • Keep the 2020 defined terms until counsel rewrites them; flag them, do not silently rename them.

    Don’t

    • Grey 13px type or justified text.
    • Collapsing legal sections into accordions.

    Desktop

    Hirer terms.

    Last updated [date]

    On this page

    1. Definitions
    2. Fees and payment

    Legal review pending (Q18). The 2020 terms define “Gigs” and “Gigees” and may not describe today’s three ways to hire.

    1. Definitions

    Hirer
    A business that posts a job or a shift on Jobility.
    Placement fee
    The fee charged when a hirer hires someone Jobility introduced.

    2. Fees and payment

    2.1 Pay per post

    A post is paid once, when it is published.

    1. The post stays live for the period shown when you post.
      1. Or until every opening on it is filled.
    2. No charge per applicant or per click.

    Phone, 390px

    Photography

    Nine working moments, one documentary grade: navy wardrobe, warm window light, people mid-task and never posing at the camera. AI-generated with ChatGPT image generation, fictional people, disclosed in the handoff (credits and placements in the handoff package). The j line never touches a photo. Parmi’s portrait stays the initials placeholder until his real photo arrives; no AI face stands in for a named person.

    Photo frame: .photo

    New in this library
    Classes
    .photo .photo--portrait .photo--tall .photo--wide .photo__note .fd-photo
    Hooks
    style="--pos:30% 35%;--pos-m:30% 30%" <picture> webp + jpg srcset 800w / 1536w width / height loading="lazy" decoding="async"
    Use when
    Every photograph on the site. A <figure class="photo"> holding one <picture>: a WebP <source> and a JPEG <img> fallback, both with an 800 and a 1536 candidate and a sizes that matches the slot, so a 1440 screen at 1x pulls the 800 file (30 to 50 KB).
    Used on
    Home (Workers, not resumes; final band), Direct hiring (who does what), Contingent labor (temp crews; what we check), Gig work (healthcare prices; what we check).
    Crop
    The frame sets the aspect (--ar, default 3:2; --portrait 4:5, --tall 2:3, --wide 16:10; every frame turns 4:3 under 768px). The image fills it with object-fit: cover; --pos is the focal point on desktop and --pos-m on phones, set per photo so no face is cut at any width.
    No layout shift
    The <img> carries its real width and height and the frame its aspect-ratio, so the box is reserved before the file arrives. Measured CLS from photos: 0 on every page at 1440 and 390.
    Loading
    loading="lazy" on every photo (none sits above the fold); use fetchpriority="high" and no lazy if one ever does. decoding="async" always.
    Review mode
    .photo__note (a .qonly chip) says “Illustrative photo, AI-generated, fictional people” when review is on; the clean page shows only the photo.
    Divi
    Image module with “Force fullwidth” off; add class photo and the --pos style to the module wrapper, or set the focal point with the module’s background position. Export both sizes from assets/img/.

    Do

    • Alt text describes the scene in one sentence: who, doing what, where.
    • One radius (--r), no border, no shadow, no overlay. The set already shares one grade; a duotone would flatten the faces.
    • Place a photo where it fills dead space beside text (a left column under a short headline) before adding a new row.

    Don’t

    • Alt text or captions that call the people Jobility customers or workers.
    • Stock clichés: handshakes, smiling at the camera, a team pointing at one screen in a glass office.
    • The j line, a ribbon or any shape over a photo.
    • An AI-generated face for a named person (Parmi, a coordinator, a customer quote).

    Desktop

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • Every frame turns 4:3 and uses --pos-m for its focal point.
    • Width is the column; nothing is hidden except the final-band photo on Home, which stays a type-only band on phones.
    Markup
    <div style="max-width:560px"><figure class="photo fd-photo " style="--pos:30% 35%;--pos-m:30% 30%"><picture><source type="image/webp" srcset="assets/img/depot-dispatcher-800.webp 800w, assets/img/depot-dispatcher.webp 1536w" sizes="560px"><img src="assets/img/depot-dispatcher-800.jpg" srcset="assets/img/depot-dispatcher-800.jpg 800w, assets/img/depot-dispatcher.jpg 1536w" sizes="560px" width="1536" height="1024" alt="A dispatcher in a headset checks the morning run sheet on a clipboard at a bus depot at sunrise, coffee in hand." loading="lazy" decoding="async"></picture><span class="photo__note qonly">Illustrative photo, AI-generated, fictional people.     Pricing

    Every price on the site reads from one data file, FD.PRICES in site.js, so a price change can never leave two numbers live. In Divi, keep the typed number as the fallback and let data-price fill it. Placement fee first, everywhere: it is the only plan where “post free” is true.

    Price tiles

    New in this library
    Classes
    .tiles .tile .tile--lead .tile-n .tile-k .tile-v .tiles-after

    Fee calculator

    New in this library
    Classes
    .qq .calc .calc-field .calc-rate .stepper .calc-rows .calc-row .calc-fig .fx .diff .calc-after
    Hooks
    data-calc data-sal-num data-sal-range data-hires data-step data-seg data-rate-seg data-custom data-out="a|m|s|dm|ds|arate|fxa|fxm|fxs" data-bar data-fx data-catch data-share data-live ?salary=&hires=&agency=
    Use when
    #calculator: your hire against an agency fee, built on the .qq frame. Both Jobility rates always show, so the 8% plan is never set alone against an agency that runs the search.
    Used on
    Pricing #calculator (all inputs); Compare #fee-math and LP1 (salary and agency quote only: drop the hires field).
    Logic
    agency = salary × agency rate × hires; managed = salary × 15% × hires; self = salary × 8% × hires. Rounded to $10. Differences in dollars only. At zero or below: “About the same as this agency fee” or “more than this agency fee”, then the link to what is included. Tap a figure to see its formula.
    Tracking
    calculator_used (salary bucket, never the exact salary), calculator_custom_rate, calculator_share.
    Held
    Until Oct 21 Q3: the “Paid hourly” switch. Until Oct 21 Q1: the role picker and a pay-per-post line. Until Q14: the cap per hire.

    Do

    • Keep the 15% agency button: an owner quoted 15% will check, and “about the same” earns trust.
    • The static worked-examples table (below) is the no-JS version.

    Don’t

    • A plan toggle that makes 8% the default.
    • “About X% less” (a percentage of a percentage).

    Desktop

    Your hire: Jobility vs. an agency fee.

    Enter the salary. We’ll show the most common agency fee, or your agency’s quote, next to both Jobility placement fees.

    $a year $20,000 to $200,000. Fees are a percentage of first-year salary.
    1 to 50

    Agency fee to compare

    %

    From 10 to 35. Agency direct-hire fees commonly ran 15 to 25%; 20% was the most common (SIA, 2021 data).

    • Most common agency fee (20%)

      $45,000 × 20% = $9,000

    • Jobility managed (15%)

      $2,250 less than the agency fee.

      $45,000 × 15% = $6,750

    • Jobility self-service (8%)

      $5,400 less than the agency fee.

      $45,000 × 8% = $3,600

    The difference is what’s included. Compare what an agency fee buys.

    Post a jobTalk to us about volume pricing

    Estimate only. In a Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms (2021 data), 20% of first-year salary was the most common direct-hire fee; commercial firms commonly charged 15 to 20% and direct-hire specialists 20 to 25%. An agency fee usually includes a recruiter who runs the search, and often a replacement guarantee. Jobility charges 8% on self-service, where you run the interviews, and 15% on Jobility managed, where our team manages the search. Extra screening is not included.

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • Inputs first, results under them; figures drop to 28px.
    • The rate buttons stay in one row of three.

    Motion components

    Motion personality: sure, warm, quick. Only the home hero and the hero visual of each offering page (offer letter, crew roster, shift card) play on their own, once, when they come into view, and then hold. Everything else moves because the visitor did something. Every effect is transform, opacity or a stroke offset.

    Easing
    draw power2.inOut (--ease-io) · enter power3.out (--ease) · land expo.out · exit power2.in (--ease-in). No bounce, no elastic, no overshoot.
    Durations
    0.2s micro (--t-micro) · 0.45s UI (--t-ui) · 0.6s land (--t-land) · 1.1 to 1.6s for a line draw.
    Motion gate
    An inline script in the <head> adds .fd-motion to <html> only when reduced motion is off. The start state of an entry moment exists only under .fd-motion. If the script that plays it has not arrived in 2.5s, the class comes off and the finished picture shows. So: no JS, slow network or reduced motion all get the finished picture, never a blank.
    Banned
    Section fade-ins, hover lift, loops, parallax, auto-advancing tabs, scroll-jacking.

    Hero line (Home)

    L3From the built pages
    Classes
    .hh .hh-grid .hh-stage .hh-card .hh-panel .hh-dot .hh-seg .hh-line--d .hh-line--m
    Hooks
    .fd-hero-tl .fd-card .fd-draw .fd-node .fd-dot .fd-dlabel .fd-panel .fd-row #hh-stage #hh-seg
    Use when
    The Home hero only: the logo’s j drawn from the visitor’s request into a short list, the dot landing as the match count. The only auto-playing signature on the site.
    Used on
    Home. (On Home the section also carries id="hero" data-hero and the heading is the page’s h1; both are removed here so the library has one h1 and the header behaves.)
    CTA by mode (r6)
    Full-time: Post a job (filled) and Talk to us. Temp and Shift are quoted by the hour, so Talk to us leads and “Post a temp job” / “Post a shift” is the outlined secondary, as on the offering pages. The phone bar follows. The panel foot links each mode to its page: See direct hiring, See temp staffing, See shift coverage ([data-hero-go]).
    Shift mode (R7)
    Shift shows the same two CNAs as the Gig work hero, both Accepted, with the count reading “2 of 2 filled”, the shift’s day (7 a.m. clock-in to 7 p.m. clock-out, a neutral rule, not a second j line) and one note. The panel is full in every mode.
    Picker CTAs (R9)
    Full-time leads with Post a job. Temp and Shift lead with the outcome: Get a temp rate (contingent-labor.html#pricing) and Get a shift rate (gig-work.html#pricing), with Post a temp job / Post a shift as the outlined secondary and on the phone bar. The lede ([data-hero-sub] [data-sub]) and the small print ([data-hero-note]) follow the pick, so Temp and Shift never argue direct-hire pricing.
    Phones and tablets (R9)
    The static j down the panel now draws once, the first time the panel is 40% on screen: the stroke runs top to bottom in 0.9s, each bead appears as the stroke reaches its row and the count ticks 0 to 3 with it. Reduced motion, ?capture=1 or no IntersectionObserver: the finished picture. A pick before it plays settles it at once.
    Plays when
    Once, when the stage is 35% in view (on phones it sits below the fold). Choosing Full-time, Temp or Shift replays it for that request: the old line retracts, the new one draws.
    Sequence
    About 4.3s: request card in 0.45s; the dot leads the line through the j 1.45s; the pen lifts in an arc and lands as the j’s dot; rows check in 0.2s apart while the count goes 1, 2, 3.
    Reduced motion
    The finished picture, instantly; the picker still swaps content.
    Needs
    GSAP 3.13 and DrawSVGPlugin, then assets/home-hero.js. Without them, or on any error, the finished picture shows. In the handoff, load GSAP after first paint or replace the draw with CSS stroke-dashoffset.

    Do

    • Keep the line in front of the request card and the dot above everything.

    Don’t

    • A second auto-playing moment on Home.
    • Replay as a loop.

    Quick quote: Which plan fits you?

    L6 + L5From the built pages
    Classes
    .qq .qq-inputs .qq-salary .qq-rows .qq-row .qq-row--lead .qq-post .qq-foot .bars .bar .fill
    Hooks
    data-quote data-sal-num data-sal-range data-tier="post|self|managed" data-out="self|managed|agency|post|postbar|postrole" data-bar data-post-cta data-nurse data-live
    Use when
    “What would one hire cost?” for the visitor’s own role and salary, on one scale: pay per post (no fee when you hire, Oct 21 Q1 answered Oct 21), the 8% and 15% placement fees (of first-year salary) and the agency’s 20%. The recommendation chooses honestly: the post if the role fills easily, the placement fee for no risk if you don’t hire, 15% if Jobility does the work. Under the rows, the talk-to-sales line.
    Used on
    Home, Direct hiring (slot C, an identical copy of Home’s div.qq[data-quote]), Pricing (with two routing rows, see Pricing).
    Notes and the citation (R9)
    The sales line, “Hiring for a role not listed?” and the source sit in .qq-notes, a third grid area under the controls from 1200px (no dead column beside the results), after the results below 1200px. The 2021 survey is cited once per screen: the agency bar and the agency row carry footnote 1 (.fn), the source line starts with it. Home and Direct keep one identical copy of the block.
    Plays when
    Only on input. Numbers tween 0.45s (power3.out); fee bars scale with the same timing.
    Reduced motion
    Numbers and bars jump to their values.
    Screen readers
    One polite announcement 0.45s after the last change, summarizing all three figures.

    Do

    • Inputs stay sticky on desktop while the rows scroll.

    Don’t

    • Query parameters on the Post buttons: the portal sign-up takes none.
    • A recommendation that hides the condition (the post is cheapest only if the role fills easily).

    Role type
    $a year $20,000 to $200,000. Each role starts at an example salary.
    Who does the work?

    Lowest cost if the role fills easily: pay per post, $150, with no fee when you hire. No risk if you don’t hire: the 8% placement fee, $3,600, only if you hire.

    What one hire at $45,000 a year costs you

    • Pay per post. Paid up front, no fee when you hire.$150
    • Placement fee, 8%. You post and schedule.$3,600
    • Placement fee, 15%. We do the work and send you a candidate.$6,750
    • Common agency fee, 20%1$9,000

    1. Pay only if you hire

      Placement fee. Nothing up front. Hourly roles: we convert the pay rate to a yearly amount.

      Post a job

    2. Or pay per post

      $150 a post for general labor and light industrial roles, no fee when you hire (second post free for new customers).

      We confirm the price before you pay.

      Post for $150See prices by role

      Choose pay per post in the job wizard when you post.

    3. Posting most months?

      Subscription. Talk to us

    Enterprise pricing, custom plans, background checks and extra verification: talk to sales.

    Hiring for a role not listed? Talk to us.

    1 Estimate only. Source: Staffing Industry Analysts survey of US staffing firms, 2021 data. 20% was the most common direct-hire fee; firms commonly charged 15 to 25%.

    Workers, not resumes: the switch

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .sw .switch .thumb .sw-canvas .sw-pile .sw-sheet .sw-cards .sw-card .sw-line .sw-state .thumb-in
    Hooks
    data-switch data-board-text data-jobility-text role="switch" aria-checked data-state="board|jobility" .fd-switch .fd-canvas .fd-sheet .fd-card .fd-draw .fd-node .fd-dot
    Use when
    Flip between what a job board sends (a pile) and what Jobility sends (three people). The finished Jobility state is the default markup.
    Used on
    Home.
    Text column (R8)
    Under the paragraph, a ruled fact pair (.wnr-facts): “No charge per click.” and “A person reviews every match.” (both already said in the paragraph), then the job boards link. From 1024px the column centres on the switch card.
    Text column (R9)
    The paragraph no longer repeats the ruled pair: “Job boards send you everyone who clicks apply, and some charge you for each one. Jobility sends a short list of people who match what you asked for, screened by our software before they reach you.” The three cards share one left edge.
    Plays when
    Only on click or arrow keys.
    Handoff
    The thumb carries a white copy of both labels and clips them as it slides, so a label is never half navy and half white. The cards leave first (0.28s), then the pile lands (from 0.3s); going back, the pile leaves first and the cards land from 0.3s. Nothing overprints.
    Reduced motion
    Instant swap between the two states.
    Build
    The j line is rebuilt from the real card positions on resize and after fonts load.

    Do

    • Keep “Illustration. Names and numbers are examples.”

    Don’t

    • Auto-flipping, or a loop between the states.

    Desktop

    Flip the switch to compare what each one sends you.

    Walkthrough tabs (v4, the j thread)

    L11From the built pages
    Classes
    .tour .tour-tabs .tour-tab .tour-panel .wt-panel .wt .wt-fit .wt-stage .wt-app .wt-view .wt-dash .wt-tiles .wt-posts .wt-prow .wt-map .wt-cd .wt-pin .wt-iv .wt-notes .wt-wiz .wt-steps .wt-pane .wt-radio .wt-wb .wt-terms .wt-cost .wt-rgrid .wt-mv .wt-mhead .wt-ptabs .wt-card .wt-mb .wt-chip--ok .wt-chip--na .wt-drawer .wt-dtabs .wt-modal .wt-x .wt-btnx .wt-ctrl .wt-seg .wt-now .wt-pause .wt-replay .tour-cap .tour-next .tour-end .tour-again .is-touch .wt-lens .wt-world .is-zoom .wt-call .wt-calln .wt-n .wt-chip--add .wt-j .wt-jl .wt-jst .wt-jpen .wt-jdot .wt-jbead .wt-jeye
    Hooks
    data-tabs data-walkthrough role="tablist|tab|tabpanel" data-tab-next .fd-wt .fd-wt-cursor .fd-wt-replay .fd-wt-pause .wt-seg[data-beat] [data-t] (cursor targets) .fd-a / .fd-m / .fd-b (swap states; .fd-b is the final frame) .fd-wt-dash / .fd-wt-mv / .fd-wt-force (views) FD.wt, FD.wtView() (QA) .fd-wt-world (the camera layer: app and cursor) .fd-wt-call1, .fd-wt-n, .fd-wt-cn (the rolling count) FD.wt[i].camIv (camera moves, QA) .fd-wt-j (one svg per view; tab 1: the app body, above the wizard) FD.wt[i].jfly (the pen's flights, QA)
    Use when
    One portal, three steps, one tab at a time. v4 (Oct 22): the brand’s j line is the thread of the whole tour. v3: a virtual camera frames each beat. v2: each tab is a stylized marketing redraw of Jobility’s real enterprise portal, matched to Kim’s two screen recordings: Night app bar, Passion actions and labels, Day surfaces, fictional data only (Warehouse supervisor, Edison, NJ; Maria R., Priya S. and James O., the Home hero’s three). Tab 1 is the real six-step wizard (Basics, What, Where, When, Terms, Review) in a drawer over the dashboard; tab 2 is the dashboard, the match list with pipeline tabs and the profile drawer; tab 3 is the Schedule interview modal, the hire and Close position. Markup _lib/wt2_build.py, CSS _lib/wt2.css, timelines _lib/wt2_tabs.js, player assets/walkthrough.js.
    Used on
    Home #walkthrough; How it works #walkthrough (larger, full captions). Tab 1’s final frame (the new post on the dashboard) is the static image for LP1 step 1.
    Tab 1 caption (integration)
    Tab 1 reads “Post your job once.” on Home and How it works (free sign-up is Q14).
    Tab 1: Post your job
    About 14.7s, five beats. Wide on the dashboard; + Add post opens the wizard (the portal’s full-height drawer; its footer follows the fields) while the camera pushes in (about 1.45x) on the template and the post title, typed to “Warehouse supervisor”. Pan to Pricing type (Placement fee) and worker type (Full time); the terms panel is held about 1.3s. Then one held wizard shot (stepper, fields and Next, about 1.35x) for What, Where (mini map, the pin drops), When (held 1.3s; Posting expires shows the portal’s own date), Terms (the cost summary opens with the step, held 1.2s; no price) and Review (held 1.2s; Post / Save as draft). The camera pulls back wide as the post takes the top row, Active ticks 4 to 5. The j thread, one thing at a time: once the wizard has gone and the new post holds the top row, the pen (a Purpose dot) appears at the row’s end and leads the ribbon line across the gutter and down into Edison; the pin drops as the pen arrives, and the line runs into the pin (the job goes out). Phones: the pin drops (no route for a line).
    Tab 2: Review vetted matches
    About 14s. Wide, then a push in on the posts and the map; each match lands as a dot and streaks a thin ribbon line into the Edison pin (none crosses a label), and the count ticks as each line arrives, to 17; a Night callout repeats the count. View 17 matches: Priya S. is the top match (98%), then Maria R. (96%) and James O. (95%). The camera frames the list and the j is written as the hero writes it: from Priya’s card down the 1.6em gutter between the name and status columns, through every card, into the curl under the last; the pen runs back up the stem and lands as the j’s dot on her card; her badge answers; then the mark fades back. Cards: BG check “Add-on” (background checks are an add-on through sales); Ref check Verified on Priya and Maria. Priya’s profile drawer (Skills & credentials). Back; her status menu; Short listed; wide on the short list. Phones and the small desktop canvas (no gutter wide enough): the streaks only.
    Tab 3: Interview and hire
    About 14.5s, six camera moves (was eight). Schedule interview: the modal. Schedule: her card, the Interviewing tab and the Dashboard nav in one shot; the pen comes out of her 98% badge, runs along the card’s top edge and down the gutter, and goes into her interview button, which turns to “Pending interview confirmation” as it arrives. Dashboard: Interviews and Notifications (Requested 2 to 3; she confirms). Her confirmation in the feed opens the list (no wide shot in between): status menu, “Interviewed Nov. 4”; the pen rises out of the button, up the gutter and into her status, which turns to Hired; Close position. Wide on the dashboard: her row reads hired, the counts move, the streets step back, and the pen leaves the Hired tag and leads the line into the Edison pin, where it becomes the pin’s white eye. Phones: the hire ends on a still ring.
    Camera (v3)
    Stage, lens, world: the stage clips and carries the frame’s corners and hairline; .wt-lens is still; .fd-wt-world (the app and the cursor) is the camera layer, moved by GSAP x / y / scale (origin 0 0, power2.inOut, 0.6 to 0.8s; one slow 1.5s drift in tab 2). A shot names the elements to frame, a scale cap (desktop up to 1.8; phones a lower cap or their own elements, pel), padding and an optional left alignment. The scale is the largest that fits, never under 1, the world always covers the stage, and the subject is never cut: the scale steps down until every line of the subject sits wholly inside the frame. While zoomed, the lens feathers the frame’s edges (.95em across, .75em top and bottom), so what a shot crops fades out instead of being cut off. Every tab opens and ends wide, so the last frame is the reduced-motion and no-GSAP frame. The cursor and tap dot live in world coordinates. will-change is set only during moves, so held text re-rasterizes crisp. Probes w3_scan.js and w3_perf.js: effective text on focus holds 15.6px or more at 1440 and 1024; the cursor inside the frame in every frame; no long tasks and no dropped frames during camera moves.
    Callout (v3)
    One editorial callout, tab 2 beat 2: a Night chip outside the camera with the rolling count in Energy, wiped in by a clip and closed upward in .2s (never faded, so it covers rather than overprints). No other annotation sits inside the product.
    The j thread (v4)
    The design direction’s hook, “one line that gathers every kind of hire and lands on the right person”, drawn the way the home hero draws it: the ribbon gradient (Optimism at the start, Passion at the end), one weight (.5em, never under 8px; the match streaks .24em), and the dot is the pen (a Purpose dot leads every line and becomes the j’s dot). One line per view; it runs only through gutters and the map, never over text (probe w4_jtext.js: 0 frames at 1440, 1024, 768 and 390, pen included); no dot is left behind as a badge; it lives in the app’s own layer, so the camera scales it; geometry from layout offsets, rebuilt on resize. Every step is a fromTo (the line’s dash offset and the pen move together in short linear steps along one ease), so seeking is exact. Reduced motion, ?capture=1, no GSAP: each tab’s final state, still (tab 3: the line in the pin).
    Player
    Unchanged from R6 to R8: plays once when the frame and its controls are fully in view; pauses off screen and resumes; Pause beside Replay while playing (WCAG 2.2.2); a tab left mid-play resumes; Next never moves the page; never auto-advances or loops; keyboard via site.js; part markers play from a part; the part now playing is announced only after a control is used.
    Handoff grammar
    Nothing overprints: text changes are out, then in, in clipped boxes (.wt-x, .wt-btnx, with .fd-a, .fd-m, .fd-b); views change out, then in; drawers slide fully opaque; the modal is a clip reveal, not a fade; menus open and close at once; a list makes room before a new row fades into the gap. Each view and card is its own stacking context. Probe: 0 overprint and 0 clipped frames at 1440, 1024 and 390.
    Phones
    A 24em canvas, 44em tall on every tab: three tiles in a row, posts, one line of notifications, the map (Newark and Edison labels), interviews; the wizard full width; match cards show two people with the checks stacked; the profile is a sheet revealed by a clip; the modal full width. A tap dot instead of an arrow. The j line is drawn only where the map sits beside the list (768px and up); phones end tab 3 on a still Passion ring around the Edison pin.
    Review round (Oct 22)
    Two art-director rounds (7.55, then 8.15). Fixed: Review held 1.9s with its blocks arriving one by one; the count as a Passion pill (“Finding matches”, then 9, then 17) while the map pushes in 1.14x on Edison with labels kept inside; the line now starts after Priya’s avatar and Hired tag and drops into the pin’s head (white eye); Priya confirms (Requested 3 to 2, Scheduled 1 to 2) before she is hired; Action required returns to 3 when the position closes; a ring marks the current wizard step; the pointer steps aside while cards arrive.
    Independent score (Oct 22, evening)
    A fresh art director scored v2 at 6.8 (v1_walk2_score.md). Fixed F2 to F19: Interviewed before Hired and Scheduled back to 1; phone status menus open upward; the pill dot has its own gutter; no 8px view jump between tabs 2 and 3; Action and Hired share a right edge; the pointer leaves labels; Where gets a mini map and When the 30-day expiry; tab 1 paced to about 16s (template preset); a taller phone map; the phone post slot opens on landing; phone step dots with labels under them; candidate ring; shorter captions; tab 2 ends on the short list; support wording and Contacting; partial fifth star; the pill grows from its left edge; terms copy and alignment. The per-beat camera (F1, structural) was not done.
    Reduced motion
    No animation: each tab shows its final frame (the markup default), Pause, Replay and the cursor hidden. Same with JavaScript off, GSAP blocked or ?capture=1; tab 3’s line is drawn, still.
    Type floor
    10.5px for every product string at every width: --xs, --s, --m are max(10.5px, n x --e), where --e is the stage em as a container length.
    Tracking
    walkthrough_tab pushed to dataLayer on each tab view.
    Larger frame (v3)
    Desktop: the frame takes the row and the caption is a narrow column (220 to 250px), so the stage is about 920px wide at 1440 (was 805) and type is 12px in the wide shot; frame and controls fit one 900px screen (the column is capped by viewport height). Under 1200px the frame and caption are centred, caption under the frame. Autoplay counts the frame as in view once the frame and its part markers are on screen.
    Small desktop canvas
    From 521 to 780px of frame width (tablets) the 10.5px floor makes cards and panes taller, so the list drops card descriptions, the wizard body is 19.6em and the status menu opens upward. Post-row meta lines end in an ellipsis before the Action select.
    Round 9
    Under 1024px the step heading sits above the frame, then the frame and controls, the lede and bullets, and Next (or Post a job) last. Autoplay waits until the controls, Pause included, are on screen. Phones: the wizard footer follows the fields and the wizard shot frames stepper to footer (about 1.12x); tab 2’s push-in on the count stops at 1.3. Tab 3: the feed click’s ripple lasts 0.1s and the pointer leaves with the dashboard, returning on Priya’s status from the right, so nothing rings on James. Pipeline counts roll in 0.2s. The lens feather is 1.6em across and 1.1em top and bottom (about 28px at 1440), and the cut test uses the same band. From 1200px the caption column is 22% (at least 240px) with a 16px lede.

    Do

    • Redraw from Jobility’s screens in the brand; never a screenshot. Fictional names, numbers and dates only.
    • Keep the portal’s own words for its controls (Pricing type, Pay per post / Subscription, Placement fee, Short listed, Accept / Close position, Schedule interview).
    • Show checks honestly: background checks as an “Add-on” chip; a reference check Verified only where the story earns it.
    • Frame each beat on its subject (name, control and result in one shot); start and end every tab wide.

    Don’t

    • The class .wt-live on anything inside the stage: it is the player’s live flag on .tour.
    • A price, 1099, a phone number, an email, a test account name, or a cert or license number anywhere in the frame.
    • A “Salary mismatch” tag, or any claim the site has not confirmed (non-refundable fees) in the terms panel or cost box.
    • Auto-advance on a timer, or loops.
    • Browser chrome or device frames around the frame.
    • A camera move while the cursor travels to a target that will leave the frame (hide the pointer and bring it back instead).
    • will-change left on the world during a hold (blurry scaled text).
    • Marketing lines dressed as product UI inside the app.
    • More than one line in a view, a line across text, or the ribbon gradient on anything but the brand line.

    Desktop

    5 of 5 Review and post

    Example view. Names and numbers are made up.

    Step 1 of 3

    Post your job once.

    Start from a template or a new post. Choose how you pay and the type of worker, add what, where and when, review, and post.

    • Pay per post, or a placement fee only if you hire; custom plans through sales
    • Full or part time on your payroll, or gig work
    • Save templates for roles you hire often

    5 of 5 Short list

    Example view. Names and numbers are made up.

    Step 2 of 3

    Your short list, already screened.

    Matches arrive on your dashboard and its map, each with a match score and ratings. Open a profile for skills and reviews, then short list.

    • Screened for skills, availability and work history
    • Every match reviewed by a person on our team
    • Background checks are an add-on through sales

    5 of 5 Your workforce

    Example view. Names and numbers are made up.

    Step 3 of 3

    Interview, choose, hire.

    Request an interview with a date, a backup time and a meeting link. After the interview, mark the hire and close the position. Every post and hire stays on one dashboard.

    • Request interviews from the match list
    • Direct hires join your payroll; gig workers can be on Jobility’s payroll
    • Every role and location in one view
    Post a job

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • Tabs become one three-part segmented control; Next sits right under the controls.
    • The product view reflows to a 24em canvas: tiles in a row, the profile as a sheet, the modal full width.
    • Captions sit below the frame.

            <p class="tour-step">Step 1 of 3</p>
            <h3>Post your job once.</h3>
            <p>Start from a template or a new post. Choose how you pay and the type of worker, add what, where and when, review, and post.</p>
            <ul><li>Pay per post, or a placement fee only if you hire; custom plans through sales</li><li>Full or part time on your payroll, or gig work</li><li>Save templates for roles you hire often</li></ul>

    Offer letter (Direct hiring hero)

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .offer .offer-top .offer-body .offer-list .offer-pick .offer-letter .ol-k .ol-to .ol-rows .ol-emp .ol-mark .xstack .xv
    Hooks
    data-offer data-seg data-name data-start data-offer-name data-offer-start data-offer-live .fd-entry .is-played .fd-offer .fd-pick .fd-letter
    Use when
    Direct hiring’s hero object: pick someone from the short list and the offer letter, from your business, is addressed to them. “Employer: your business” is the point.
    Used on
    Direct hiring hero. Its paper-and-ink language carries into the “From short list to day one” timeline.
    Entry (the page’s one)
    Once, 40% in view: the letter settles in (0.5s opacity, 0.6s rise of 14px, power3.out), then a Day fill marks “Your business” (scaleX, 0.45s). The name is in the letter from the first frame.
    Brand line (R5)
    One piece of the j line (.oj, built by jline()) leaves the chosen card and ends under the letter’s Day one row, drawn once after the employer fill by a transform-only wipe (0.85s), then still. A pick moves its start to the new card without a redraw. See Offering hero on Night Shift.
    Reduced motion
    Instant swap.
    Needs
    assets/offering.js after site.js.
    On a pick
    The name and start date swap in a masked box (old out 0.14s, new in 0.22s); the line’s start glides to the new person 0.15s later, over 0.3s.

    Do

    • Shortlist chips stay “Skills match” until Q14 confirms more.

    Don’t

    • A salary figure in the letter.
    • A second line, or a line that redraws on every pick.

    Desktop

    Your shortlist: warehouse supervisor

    Choose who gets the offer.

    Offer goes to Maria R. from your business. Day one: on your payroll.

    Offer of employment

    To Maria R.

    Position
    Warehouse supervisor, full-time
    Employer
    Your business
    Payroll
    Yours, from day one
    Day one
    Monday, in 2 weeks
    Signed

    No markup on their wages, because the person works for you, not for us.

    Example view. Names and numbers are made up.

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • The letter stacks under the list.

    Crew roster (Contingent labor hero)

    New in this library
    Classes
    .roster .rs-card .rs-head .rs-emp .rs-mark .ps-mark .rs-grid .rs-c .rs-off .is-now .rs-foot
    Hooks
    data-roster .fd-entry .fd-roster .is-played
    Use when
    Contingent labor’s hero object: weeks 1 to 3 of a six-week booking, temps on site each day, the current week in Night, the employer named on the roster. It is a planning object the temp buyer already keeps, and it leaves the pay stub to the signature section, so the page never shows the same object twice.
    Used on
    Contingent labor hero.
    Entry (the page’s one)
    Once, 40% in view: the day cells fade up week by week (0.16s between weeks, 0.03s between days, 0.3s each), then a Day fill marks “Jobility is the employer” (0.45s from 0.75s). About 1.2s in all.
    Reduced motion
    The filled roster, instantly. Without JS: filled.

    Do

    • Label it “Example view. Names and numbers are made up.” (.ex-note, the one disclaimer string).
    • One Q16 mark, on the employer.

    Don’t

    • Pay rates or a bill total (Q14).
    • The pay stub in the hero.

    • The week labels stay on one line; cells narrow to fit six days.

    On the toggle

    Shift card (Gig work hero)

    From the built pages
    Classes
    .shiftcard .sc-card .sc-head .sc-count .sc-n .sc-slots .sc-slot .sc-open .sc-filled .sc-state .sc-foot
    Hooks
    data-shiftfill data-sc-live .fd-entry .is-played .fd-shift .fd-slot .fd-bline .fd-draw .fd-dot .fd-node
    Use when
    An open Saturday CNA shift fills with two screened workers. Gig work’s one entry moment.
    Used on
    Gig work hero.
    Entry (the page’s one)
    Once, 40% in view: a 0.6s beat on the open shift, then the two slots fill 0.32s apart and the count ticks as each one lands. The visible number is an aria-hidden clone; the real “2 of 2 filled” never changes, and its aria-live switches on only after the entry.
    Reduced motion
    Filled, instantly. Without JS: filled.
    Brand line (R5)
    The shift’s arc across the day (.sc-day): one piece of the j line from the 7 a.m. clock-in bead to the Purpose dot at 7 p.m., drawn once (0.85s wipe) before the slots fill. Replaces the plain rail.
    No overprint
    Each open slot clears in 0.14s before its worker settles in, so the two layers never show at once.
    In this library
    The entry runs on a local .fd-motion class on this demo, because the home hero above clears the page-level class when it plays. Use Replay to watch it again.

    Do

    • Credential chips stay unticked until each check is confirmed (Q8, Q14).

    Don’t

    • A shift rate on the card (Q14).
