    Offering heroes and bands (R5)

    Round 5 of the offering pages. Each page opens on a Night Shift hero band whose hero object carries one piece of the j line doing one page-specific job, then one full-bleed photo band. Three surface tiers, each with one meaning: product objects keep the card and the one shadow; facts are ruled type pairs; navigation is a flat segmented bar. CSS: site.css, “R5 offering heroes and bands” (the last section).

    Offering hero on Night Shift, with one piece of the j line

    New in this library
    Classes
    .oh--night .on-dark .hd-dark .oj .oj-path .oj-wipe .oj-bead .oj-dot .sc-day .ol-day .oj-end
    Hooks
    data-jline="offer|roster|shift" .fd-jline .fd-entry .fd-draw .fd-dot .fd-node html.fd-motion .oh-sub--m / .oh-sub--d data-v-healthcare html.fd-v-healthcare ?v=healthcare
    Use when
    The first screen of Direct hiring, Contingent labor and Gig work. The header joins the band: the header markup is wrapped in <div class="hd-dark on-dark">, which is display:contents, so the nav stays sticky. The hero section is section.oh.oh--night.on-dark; its stage has no panel of its own, so the white product objects sit straight on Night.
    Used on
    Direct hiring (offer letter), Contingent labor (crew roster), Gig work (shift card). Live on each page; the hero objects themselves are under Motion components.
    The line, per page
    Direct hiring: it leaves the chosen person and ends under the letter’s Day one row; the line becomes the hire (phones: a hook under “Day one” in the one-line offer). Contingent labor: it marks This week on the roster. Gig work: it is the shift’s arc across the day, 7 a.m. clock-in bead to 7 p.m. clock-out dot.
    How it is built
    offering.js jline(host, route) appends one SVG over the object and draws a cubic path in the object’s own pixels: ribbon gradient (Optimism, Energy, Purpose, Passion) along the path, a Night bead at the start where the line starts on something, the Purpose dot (the j’s dot) at the end. A ResizeObserver rebuilds it; it never redraws.
    Facts
    .oh-facts on Night are ruled type pairs: a 2px Day rule, label in Day at 84%, value in white 800. No boxes.
    Mode strip
    On the Night band .doors--strip is a flat segmented bar; the current page is the Day segment.
    Weight
    One weight family: --oj-w 12px from 768px, 9px on phones (Home’s stroke is 20px). Contingent: the Week 2 to Week 3 gap opens by 22px (transform only) so the line runs clear of both rows, and the dot ends in the gutter right of the grid.
    Phones
    Under 768px: h1, a one-sentence lede (.oh-sub--m), the buttons, then the hero object. The facts dl and the long lede (.oh-sub--d) wait for wider screens.
    Small print
    One line under the hero buttons at most. Contingent and Gig work: “Tell us the roles, headcount and dates.” (hidden on phones).
    Contingent hook (R8)
    Along the opened This week gap, down a channel inside the card’s right edge (46px; 30px on phones) with an open top corner at the logo’s radius (up to 20px), then a bowl that runs left under week 3 and lifts as it ends between the last two columns, like the j’s tail, into the dot. It sits 16px under the cells; the grid keeps 60px under week 3 (58px on phones) so the stroke clears the footer rule by 24px or more.
    Healthcare landing
    gig-work.html?v=healthcare swaps the eyebrow, headline and both ledes, and every primary on the page reads Get a CNA shift rate with data-v-healthcare-role="cna": the hero, the final band and the phone sticky bar. Links go to contact.html?topic=shift&role=cna&src=healthcare plus any utm_* (FD.srcQ()). The inline script after the hero swaps the hero (no flash); site.js applies the variant again to anything after it (final band, sticky bar) before it writes the contact links.
    Entry (once, 40% in view)
    The line is created already held (is-pre is-snap: transitions off for its first style resolution), so it never shows drawn and then rewinds. Direct: the letter settles, the employer fill runs, then the line wipes in (0.85s) and its dot lands. Contingent: the days fill week by week, then the line marks This week. Gig: the arc wipes in, then the slots fill. Transform only.
    Reduced motion and failsafes
    The finished line, no wipe. No IntersectionObserver, a late script (2.5s gate) or an object that never scrolls into view (4s): the finished picture.
    Pick (direct)
    The new name arrives first; 150ms later the line’s start glides to the new person over 0.3s (power2.inOut, path, bead and gradient rebuilt each frame), never a jump.

    Do

    • One line per view, on the hero object only.
    • Keep the line under or beside text, never through it.
    • AA on Night: body in Day (11:1), labels in Day at 84% (8:1 or better).

    Don’t

    • The line on a photo, in a CTA band or on a second object on the same page.
    • A line that loops, pulses or redraws on interaction.
    • A Day panel behind the hero object on the Night band (the object is the card).

    Photo band: .pband (one per page, Home included)

    New in this library
    Classes
    .pband .pband-frame .fd-pband .photo__note
    Hooks
    style="--pos:50% 15%;--pos-m:56% 40%" <picture> webp + jpg srcset 800w / 1536w / 3072w, sizes 100vw width / height loading="lazy" .pband--home
    Use when
    One full-bleed working moment per offering page, right after the hero: 520px tall at 1440 (clamp 300 to 520), 300px on phones, no radius, no overlay, a one-line caption under it in the column. The frame has a fixed height, so nothing shifts while it loads.
    Used on
    Home (dispatcher, between the agency rows and Workers, not resumes), Direct hiring (bakery), Contingent labor (overcast site walk), Gig work (staffing coordinator, board cleaned of pseudo-text).
    Crop
    object-fit:cover. Set --pos so the highest head has about 40px of air at 1440 (bakery 15%, foreman 14%, coordinator 9%, dispatcher 3% on Y); check 1024, 1440 and 1920, and --pos-m at 390.
    Resolution
    A 3072w source (Lanczos 2x plus a light unsharp mask, WebP q75, 170 to 260 KB) so the band is sharp on a 2x screen; lazy, below the fold.
    One photo, one page
    A band photo never appears anywhere else on the site. At most one boxed .photo elsewhere on an offering page, only where it fills a real column.

    Do

    • Caption in one plain line that says what the page promises.

    Don’t

    • Text, the j line or a gradient over the photo.
    • A second band on the same page.

    Desktop

    Phone, 390px

    What changes on a phone

    • 300px tall, focal point from --pos-m.

    Get a rate: the request preview (Gig work and Contingent labor)

    New in this library
    Classes
    .rate-ask .ra-l .ra-card .ra-k .ra-fields .ra-h .choices .choice .input .micro .ra-hc .ra-hc-k
    Hooks
    data-rate-ask data-rate-kind="shift|temp" data-layout="split" data-rate-link name="rate-role|zip|len|n|start|end" .fd-rate-ask .rate-cta .fd-rate-cta
    Use when
    No hourly rate is published (Q14). Left: heading, lede and the role chips (and on Contingent labor the overtime and sales lines). Right, a request card: what we need for a rate. Shift: ZIP code and shift length (8, 10 or 12 hours). Temp: ZIP code, headcount, start and end dates. The filled button names the role (“Get a CNA shift rate”, “Get a forklift operator rate”) and carries every value as query parameters: contact.html?topic=shift&role=cna&zip=08820&len=12, contact.html?topic=temp&role=forklift&zip=08820&n=4&start=&end=, plus src=healthcare and any utm_* from the landing. Nothing is submitted on the page: the button is a link to the contact form (the prototype shows its polite note), and Enter in a field presses it. The ZIP hint says “We’ll confirm coverage for this ZIP.”
    Used on
    Gig work (#pricing, Day band) and Contingent labor (#pricing, white band).
    Healthcare note (R8)
    Gig work only: under the card, a ruled “For healthcare facilities” note says license and background checks for nursing roles are set up with the account (Q8 mark in review). Licenses left the “What we check” list, which now holds only the standard, ticked checks; the Q14 note stays review-only.
    CTAs and chips (R9)
    The contingent and gig heroes and phone bars lead with Get a temp rate / Get a shift rate (to #pricing), Post a temp job / Post a shift outlined beside. Small print says what the card asks for. Contingent adds a General laborer chip (the crews now include job sites). Under 420px a label longer than 24 characters becomes “Get this rate” (the chip names the role; the full label is the aria-label). On ?v=healthcare the hero, phone bar and final band go to the form, and a chip pick (LPN, RN, Med aide) updates their role and label (“Get an LPN shift rate”); the final band reads “Cover your next CNA or nurse shift.”

    Do

    • One filled primary in the band; the phone sticky bar hides while the band is on screen.
    • Two columns from 1024px; under it the card follows the chips and the price lines come last.

    Don’t

    • A number, a range or “from $” until Q14.
    • A fake “sent” state. The contact form is where the request is made.

      Staffing agencyJobility
      Price20% of first-year salary, the most common fee18% or 15% of first-year salary, or a flat price per post
      When you payWhen you hirePlacement fee: when you hire. Per post: when you publish

          <tr><th scope="row">Price</th><td data-label="Staffing agency">20% of first-year salary, the most common fee<sup class="fn">1</sup></td><td class="is-us" data-label="Jobility">8% or 15% of first-year salary, or a flat price per post</td></tr>

    </div>

      Motion and whimsy: the pen

      The dot is the pen, everywhere. The home hero teaches it (a Purpose dot leads the j line and lands as the match); these components make it the site’s handwriting at small scale. Links are written by the pen, toggles glide, and a few places get one drawn loop. (A button tap dot was tried and cut after the 2026-10-23 whimsy review: nobody could see it under a finger or a click.) Everything here ends in under 1.0s, runs once, never loops, and its finished state is the markup. Reduced motion, ?capture=1, the site motion switch (Accessibility page) and no JavaScript all show the finished, still picture. CSS: site.css “Whimsy P1”; JS: site.js “Whimsy P1”.

      Pen link

      S1New in this library
      Classes
      .fd-pen-ink .fd-pen-a .is-multi
      Hooks
      --pen-y
      Use when
      Every text link in page content (main a, .tlink, .links-row a) that has a visible resting underline. No markup change: site.js wraps the link’s text in span.fd-pen-ink and sets --pen-y from the real baseline and the link’s own underline offset, so the stroke sits exactly on the underline.
      Used on
      Every page. Not in the header, footer, menus, the LP header and footer, the phone bar, the home hero, the walkthrough, the Workers switch, links marked aria-current, buttons, or links whose resting underline is transparent (they have their own hover rule).
      Plays when
      Hover or keyboard focus. A 2px stroke in the link’s own color draws left to right over the resting underline (0.2s, power2.inOut), led by a 6px Purpose dot (the j’s dot, on every link whatever its color) that holds 0.12s at the stroke’s end, then fades in 0.15s. Leaving clears it in place (0.15s fade), never a reverse wipe.
      Multi-line links
      The underline goes to 2px, no stroke and no dot.
      Reduced or calm
      The underline goes to 2px at once.

      Do

      • Keep the resting underline: it is the affordance; the pen only adds to it.

      Don’t

      • A pen on a link that already marks the current page.
      • Hover lift or color fills on text links.

      Read how Jobility works, or compare the fee with a staffing agency.

      See pricesSee all questions

      Markup
      <p class="lede" style="max-width:56ch">Read <a href="how-it-works.html">how Jobility works</a>, or compare the fee with <a href="compare-staffing-agency.html">a staffing agency</a>.</p><p class="links-row"><a class="tlink" href="pricing.html">See prices</a><a class="tlink" href="faq.html">See all questions</a></p>

      The loop (shared recipe)

      S2, S3 + Package 2New in this library
      Classes
      .fd-loop .fd-loop-host .fd-loop-path .fd-loop-dot .fd-loop-word .fd-receipt
      Hooks
      FD.loopAround(el, opts) FD.drawLine(path, opts) FD.loopGeo(box, opts) FD.crPath(points)
      Use when
      The brand guide’s “loop around a word, sparingly”. For a box w x h: pad 10px across and 8px up and down; a hand-drawn ellipse that starts lower left, overshoots its start by about 20% of the perimeter, and exits up and right into a short tail that ends on the j’s Purpose dot (4px, 3px on phones). Stroke 3px, round caps, ribbon gradient along x (Optimism to Passion), pathLength="1", drawn by stroke-dashoffset 1 to 0 once. aria-hidden, no pointer events, never rewinds. The SVG fills its host (absolute, overflow:visible), so it never shifts layout.
      Used on
      Talk to us thank-you (“in touch”, 0.15s after the reveal; side pad 0.1em so it clears “be”; loop 0.65s, dot 0.25s), the footer egg (no dot, fades away; 0.12em wider and 0.08em taller than the word so it clears its letters), the 404 (“Looking for {page}?”). Package 2: the $0 on Pricing.
      D1 rule
      On a copy page, at most one small loop or line, drawn once, then still; never in a CTA band, never on a photo, never in the same view as another line.
      Reduced or calm
      Drawn and still at first paint.

      Desktop

      Thanks. We’ll be in touch.

      Markup
      <h3 class="h-sub" style="position:relative">Thanks. We’ll be in <span>touch</span>.</h3><p style="margin-top:14px"><button class="btn btn--secondary" type="button" onclick="var h=this.parentNode.previousElementSibling;[].forEach.call(h.querySelectorAll('.fd-loop'),function(s){s.remove()});FD.loopAround(h.querySelector('span'),{host:h,draw:true})">Draw the loop</button></p>

      404: the pen loops the next page

      S2New in this library
      Classes
      .fd-nf .fd-nf-line .fd-nf-hit
      Hooks
      ?from= page_not_found_suggest
      Use when
      0.4s after load the pen loops the best next link once (0.65s) and lands as the j’s Purpose dot at its tail (0.25s). The loop clears the words’ corners and stops 6px short of the screen edge and 4px short of the next link. (A routed line from the H1’s stop to the link was cut after the 2026-10-23 review: with right-angle corners it read as a flowchart.) The best next link is chosen before first paint by an inline script: the IA’s 301 list (/home/, /part-time-work/, /gigee-video/ to For workers; /hirer-video/ to How it works; /careers/joblist/, /apply/, /jd-listing/ to Careers; /exclusive-offer/, /offer-claimed/ to Pricing; /contact-us/, /demo-request/ to the contact form), then the nearest page slug. A hit is inserted first in the link row as “Looking for {page}?”; a miss draws nothing (the two buttons are already the answer). In the prototype the old path rides on ?from=/old-path/; live it is the request path.
      Used on
      404.html?from=/pricing-plans/, ?from=/careers/joblist/, no match.
      Reduced or calm
      Drawn and still.
      No JS
      No loop, no suggestion; the links are already there.

      The motion contract (helpers for every package)

      D2New in this library
      Classes
      html.fd-calm --ease-land
      Hooks
      FD.reduced() FD.setMotion(on) FD.motionOn() fd:motion FD.EASE FD.drawLine() FD.loopAround() localStorage fd-motion
      Use when
      FD.reduced() is true under prefers-reduced-motion, the site motion switch (localStorage fd-motion = off, html.fd-calm) or ?capture=1. FD.setMotion(on) writes the setting, toggles html.fd-calm and fires fd:motion. Under html.fd-calm every transition and animation is instant (as under reduced motion). The home hero and the walkthrough honour it (one hook each in home-hero.js and walkthrough.js); the offering lines follow FD.reduced(). Pages built by Package 1 set html.fd-calm in the head before first paint, so no start state is ever held; the others get it from site.js before anything plays.
      drawLine
      FD.drawLine(path, {dur, delay, ease, dot, dotDur, pen}) draws once (WAAPI on stroke-dashoffset) and returns a Promise. dot lands after the line (0.25s, expo.out); pen:true rides the dot on the line’s head, then lands it. Reduced: finished at once, resolved.
      loopAround
      FD.loopAround(el, {host, draw, dur, delay, dot, tail, fade, padX, padY, start, sweep, rect}) builds the loop over the last line box of el inside host, rebuilt on resize and font load; returns the SVG (svg.fdPath, svg.fdDot, svg.fdDone).
      Easing
      FD.EASE.io draw, .enter, .land (= --ease-land, expo.out), .exit.

      Motion and whimsy: the copy pages

      One owned object per copy page, made of the brand’s dot and line, each drawn once and then still. They follow the pen’s rules (group above): under 1.0s per move, no loops, no hover lift, the finished picture is the markup, and reduced motion, ?capture=1, the motion switch and no JavaScript all show it at once. CSS assets/whimsy-p2.css, JS assets/whimsy-p2.js, linked only from the pages that use them. After the 2026-10-23 review the FAQ read map was cut (it read as carousel dots).
